Title: Hohe geistreiche Lehren vnd Erklärungen vber die fürnembsten Sprüche dess Hohen Lieds Salomonis von der Liebhabenden Seele, das ist, der Christlichen Kirchen (title as catalogued by the host; the OCR of the engraved title page is unreadable)
Creator: Daniel Sudermann (1550-1631?), of Strasbourg, follower of Caspar Schwenckfeld; engraved figures by Jacob van der Heyden
Publisher: Colophon: 'Gedruckt zu Franckfurt bey Eberhardt Kiefer, in Verlegung Jacobs von der Heyden, Chalcograph'; the host catalogue gives [Strasbourg]; Frankfurt
Publication Date: 1622 (host catalogue; the colophon's year is OCR'd as 'Anno N De XXIII', a seventeenth-century date either way)
Rights: Public Domain (a seventeenth-century printing, public domain by date; the host item carries no rights field and is not access-restricted)
Language: deu
Source: Internet Archive item hohegeistreichel00sude, file hohegeistreichel00sude_djvu.txt (Getty Research Institute copy), fetched directly by the library/source-research thread (Era VII session, one-world agent for schwenckfelders), 2026-10-16; host rights statement: none on the item record (no possible-copyright-status field; collections getty, americana; contributor Getty Research Institute).
Provenance note: Header prepended; long-s to s normalisation: 10603 occurrences replaced; the OCR text otherwise follows unchanged. Rights basis restated: a printing of the 1620s is public domain by date.
Content note: Sudermann's verse-and-prose meditations on the Song of Songs, verse by verse ('im Hohen Lied Salomonis am 1. Cap.' and so on), each with an engraved figure, opening 'Vom Hohen Lied Salomonis schreibet ein Christlicher Lehrer' and closing with an errata list ('Correctur etlicher Wörter nach dem Original Exemplar') and the colophon. The one Schwenkfelder-community voice inside the census window (1540s-1650) found on a reachable host. SECOND WITNESS for scan quality (Mark's OCR ruling, 2026-09-25): Fraktur OCR sampled at five points, whole-word share 0.74-0.79, with the title page and the engravings' lettering lost; quote only after checking the page images. No English translation exists.
